## Details

Per-module seeders that write the demo dataset's rows (accounts, addresses, products, blank, example) into a database through the shared SeedRepository / OwnedSeedRepository primitive, using insertIfAbsent / insertIfAbsentForOwner so re-runs skip rather than rewrite and pre-save hooks (e.g. password hashing) still run.

### Runtime & Kernel Backbone [[Expand]](./Runtime_Kernel_Backbone.md)
The shared runtime and kernel services the seeding subsystem executes against — the module registry that resolves repositories, the outbox kernel (event enqueue/relay), the translation kernel (Translatable, TranslationPort, applyTranslations), the ability/permission kernel (permitsOnRows), rate-limiting, Redis, the image worker, and the development doubles (e.g. registerOAuthProvider('fake')) that stand in for external systems during seeding. This is the substrate every seeder and flow action depends on; it is not seeding-specific but is the fixed environment the fixtures are written into.

**Related Classes/Methods**:

- `src.kernel.outbox.enqueueOutboxEvent`:160-170
- `src.kernel.translation.TranslationPort`:60-166
- `src.kernel.translation.applyTranslations`:371-391
- `src.kernel.ability.permitsOnRows`:218-236

### Kernel Write-Surface Primitives [[Expand]](./Kernel_Write_Surface_Primitives.md)
The transactional and contract-level write primitives that seeders invoke to produce the same side effects a real service call would — transactional outbox announcements (announceInTransaction, announceWithin), the translation write plan (TranslationWritePlan, applyTranslations.resolved), the access query collapse, and the module-level PII/email helpers (e.g. encryptOrderPii, decryptOrderAddress, withdrawalCase). Seeders deliberately route through these primitives (e.g. planTranslations/writeTranslations) rather than raw driver writes so that the seeded rows are indistinguishable from rows a human editor would have produced.

**Related Classes/Methods**:

- `src.kernel.outbox.announceInTransaction`:492-499
- `src.kernel.outbox.announceWithin`:471-479
- `src.kernel.access.query.collapse`:122-135
- `src.modules.orders.pii.encryptOrderPii`:78-90
- `src.modules.orders.pii.decryptOrderAddress`:97-104

### Module Repositories & Domain Services [[Expand]](./Module_Repositories_Domain_Services.md)
The per-module repository and service layer that seeders write through — the SeedRepository / OwnedSeedRepository structural interface is satisfied by these repositories (e.g. src.modules.account.repository with deleteById, findLiveFamilyRow, issueVerificationToken, removeAllSessions, removeAllVerificationTokens), alongside the supporting domain services (password hashing verifyPassword/Derivation, mail budget, roles, cache, upload, HTTP response normalisation). This is the seam where the seeding primitive meets each bounded context: the seeder never touches the driver directly, it hands a fixture to the module's own create(), which is what guarantees pre-save hooks (password hashing, PII encryption) still fire.

**Related Classes/Methods**:

- `src.infrastructure.security.password-hash.verifyPassword`:106-121
- `src.modules.account.mail-budget.mailRecipientRefusal`:60-71
- `src.infrastructure.http.response.normalizeErrors`:230-258

### Per-Module Seeders & Scenario Entry Points [[Expand]](./Per_Module_Seeders_Scenario_Entry_Points.md)
The heart of the subsystem — the shared seeding primitive and the per-module seeders that compose it. scenarios/seed.ts defines SeedRepository, OwnedSeedRepository, insertIfAbsent (keyed by pinned _id) and insertIfAbsentForOwner (keyed by userId for carts/wishlists/address books). Each module file (accounts.ts, addresses.ts, products.ts, example.ts, blank.ts, locales.ts, …) declares its fixtures with pinned ids, exposes a seed*Collection function, and registers a shopModule entry (regenerated into shop-modules.generated.ts). The scenario entry points seedBlank and seedExampleCollection (and the shop runner) orchestrate the waves: access model first, then users + baseline modules concurrently, then domain modules in dependency order. This is the only group that is the seeding subsystem proper; the others are its environment and consumers.

**Related Classes/Methods**:

- `scenarios.seed.insertIfAbsent`:60-64
- `scenarios.seed.insertIfAbsentForOwner`:76-80
- `scenarios.products.seedProductsCollection`:510-520
- `scenarios.accounts.seedAccessModel`:154-171
- `scenarios.blank.seedBlank`:24-32
- `scenarios.example.seedExampleCollection`:70-71
- `scenarios.addresses.seedAddressBooksCollection`:103-106

### Flow Runner Actions (Seeded-Data Consumers) [[Expand]](./Flow_Runner_Actions_Seeded_Data_Consumers_.md)
The flow-runner action layer that exercises the seeded dataset end-to-end — the typed action surface (CartSummaryData, CheckoutData, FulfillmentData, PaymentData, Line) and the journey verbs (openPayment, holdOrder, deliverOrder, cancelOrder, cancelRemainder, hardDeleteProduct, readFulfillment, backdate, reencrypt, shop-history). These actions are the read side of the seeding subsystem: they address the pinned ids that the seeders wrote (e.g. SEED_USER_ID, SEED_ADMIN_ID, the seeded product ids) and drive the cart → checkout → payment → fulfilment → returns pipeline against the seeded rows. They also include the operational helpers (generate-role-matrix, reencrypt, backdate) that mutate the seeded state to reach specific journey preconditions.

**Related Classes/Methods**:

- `scenarios.flows.actions.openPayment`:128-129
- `scenarios.flows.actions.holdOrder`:292-300
- `scenarios.flows.actions.deliverOrder`:308-314
- `scenarios.flows.actions.cancelOrder`:346-347
- `scenarios.flows.actions.cancelRemainder`:323-340
- `scenarios.flows.actions.hardDeleteProduct`:405-406
